\vec{\imath}
. It would be very interesting to understand this further and complete the proof of Conjecture
2.105
along these lines.

B.2
Evidence for Conjecture
4.7

A possible proof of Conjecture
4.7
follows the same line of reasoning as in Appendix
B.1
, starting with the dimensional reduction (
4.3
) according to Proposition
3.63
. We choose
\vec{r}=(1,0,\dots,0)
without loss of generality. We can assume that

\displaystyle\begin{split}Z_{[\mathbbm{C}^{2}/\mathbbm{Z}_{n}]\times\mathbbm{C}^{2}}(\vec{\mathtt{q}};\vec{\epsilon},m)&=M\big((-1)^{n}\,\mathtt{Q}\big)^{-n\,\frac{m\,\epsilon_{12}\,\epsilon_{13}\,\epsilon_{23}}{\epsilon_{1}\,\epsilon_{2}\,\epsilon_{3}\,\epsilon_{4}}-\frac{n^{2}-1}{n}\,\frac{m\,\epsilon_{12}}{\epsilon_{1}\,\epsilon_{2}}}\\
&\quad\,\times\prod_{0<p\leq s<n}\,\widetilde{M}\big((-1)^{p-s+1}\,\mathtt{q}_{[p,s]},(-1)^{n}\,\mathtt{Q}\big)^{-\frac{m\,\epsilon_{12}}{\epsilon_{3}\,\epsilon_{4}}}\ G(\vec{\mathtt{q}};\vec{\epsilon},m)\end{split}\ ,

(B.21)

where the function
G
has a power series expansion

\displaystyle G(\vec{\mathtt{q}};\vec{\epsilon},m)=1+\sum_{\vec{k}\in\mathbbm{Z}_{\geq 0}^{n}\setminus\,\vec{0}}\,\vec{\mathtt{q}}^{\,\vec{k}}\ G^{(\vec{k}\,)}(\vec{\epsilon},m)

(B.22)

whose coefficients are rational functions of the equivariant parameters
with
G^{(\vec{k}\,)}(\vec{\epsilon},m=-\epsilon_{123})=0
.

The idea is to proceed by induction on the size of
\vec{k}\in\mathbbm{Z}_{\geq 0}^{n}\setminus\vec{0}
to show that
G^{(\vec{k}\,)}(\vec{\epsilon},m)=0
. For
|\vec{k}\,|=1
the only contribution to the instanton partition function is

\displaystyle\mathtt{q}_{0}\,\frac{m\,\epsilon_{12}}{\epsilon_{3}\,\epsilon_{4}}\ .

(B.23)

Thus
G^{(\vec{k}\,)}(\vec{\epsilon},m)=0
for all
\vec{k}
of size one. Now suppose
G^{(\vec{k}\,)}(\vec{\epsilon},m)=0
for all
\vec{k}
of sizes
1\leq|\vec{k}\,|\leq k-1
with
k>1
. Then for
\vec{k}
of size
|\vec{k}\,|=k
we can write

\displaystyle Z^{\vec{k}}_{[\mathbbm{C}^{2}/\mathbbm{Z}_{n}]\times\mathbbm{C}^{2}}(\vec{\epsilon},m)=F^{(\vec{k}\,)}(\vec{\epsilon},m)+G^{(\vec{k}\,)}(\vec{\epsilon},m)\ ,

(B.24)

where
F^{(\vec{k}\,)}(\vec{\epsilon},m)
is the coefficient of
\vec{\mathtt{q}}^{\,\vec{k}}
in the power series expansion of

\displaystyle M\big((-1)^{n}\,\mathtt{Q}\big)^{-n\,\frac{m\,\epsilon_{12}\,\epsilon_{13}\,\epsilon_{23}}{\epsilon_{1}\,\epsilon_{2}\,\epsilon_{3}\,\epsilon_{4}}-\frac{n^{2}-1}{n}\,\frac{m\,\epsilon_{12}}{\epsilon_{1}\,\epsilon_{2}}}\ \prod_{0<p\leq s<n}\,\widetilde{M}\big((-1)^{p-s+1}\,\mathtt{q}_{[p,s]},(-1)^{n}\,\mathtt{Q}\big)^{-\frac{m\,\epsilon_{12}}{\epsilon_{3}\,\epsilon_{4}}}\ .

(B.25)

From the matrix integral (
3.62
) with
\vec{r}=(1,0,\dots,0)
for the
\mathbbm{Z}_{n}
-action of Section
4.1
, with weights
s_{1}=1
,
s_{2}=n-1
and
s_{3}=s_{4}=0
, it follows that the fractional instanton contribution
Z^{\vec{k}}_{[\mathbbm{C}^{2}/\mathbbm{Z}_{n}]\times\mathbbm{C}^{2}}(\vec{\epsilon},m)
is invariant under the permutation of
\epsilon_{3}
and
\epsilon_{4}=-\epsilon_{123}
. From the combinatorial expansion (
4.2
) it follows that it can be decomposed into

\displaystyle Z^{\vec{k}}_{[\mathbbm{C}^{2}/\mathbbm{Z}_{n}]\times\mathbbm{C}^{2}}(\vec{\epsilon},m)=\sum_{\imath=1}^{|\vec{k}\,|}\,(m+\epsilon_{123})^{\imath}\ \mathfrak{Z}^{\vec{k};\imath}_{[\mathbbm{C}^{2}/\mathbbm{Z}_{n}]\times\mathbbm{C}^{2}}(\vec{\epsilon}\,)+(-1)^{|\vec{k}\,|+k_{0}}\,Z^{\vec{k}}_{[\mathbbm{C}^{2}/\mathbbm{Z}_{n}]\times\mathbbm{C}}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{3})\ ,

(B.26)

with some functions
\mathfrak{Z}^{\vec{k};\imath}_{[\mathbbm{C}^{2}/\mathbbm{Z}_{n}]\times\mathbbm{C}^{2}}(\vec{\epsilon}\,)
for
\imath=1,\dots,|\vec{k}\,|
which are independent of the mass
m
.

The powers of the generalized MacMahon functions in (
B.25
) can be rewritten respectively as

\displaystyle-n\,\frac{\epsilon_{12}\,\epsilon_{13}\,\epsilon_{23}}{\epsilon_{1}\,\epsilon_{2}\,\epsilon_{3}}+\frac{n^{2}-1}{n}\,\frac{\epsilon_{12}\,\epsilon_{123}}{\epsilon_{1}\,\epsilon_{2}}-\Big(n\,\frac{\epsilon_{12}\,\epsilon_{13}\,\epsilon_{23}}{\epsilon_{1}\,\epsilon_{2}\,\epsilon_{3}\,\epsilon_{4}}-\frac{n^{2}-1}{n}\,\frac{\epsilon_{12}}{\epsilon_{1}\,\epsilon_{2}}\Big)\,(m+\epsilon_{123})

(B.27)

and

\displaystyle-\frac{\epsilon_{12}}{\epsilon_{3}}-\frac{\epsilon_{12}\,(m+\epsilon_{123})}{\epsilon_{3}\,\epsilon_{4}}\ .

(B.28)

Since
F^{(\vec{k}\,)}(\vec{\epsilon},m)
is symmetric under
\epsilon_{3}\leftrightarrow-\epsilon_{123}
, it follows from (
B.24
) that so is
G^{(\vec{k}\,)}(\vec{\epsilon},m)
, and that it is given as the Taylor expansion

\displaystyle G^{(\vec{k}\,)}(\vec{\epsilon},m)=\sum_{\imath=1}^{|\vec{k}\,|}\,\big(\mathfrak{Z}_{[\mathbbm{C}^{2}/\mathbbm{Z}_{n}]\times\mathbbm{C}^{2}}^{\vec{k};\imath}(\vec{\epsilon}\,)-\mathfrak{F}^{(\vec{k}\,);\imath}(\vec{\epsilon}\,)\big)\,(m+\epsilon_{123})^{\imath}\ ,

(B.29)

with some functions
\mathfrak{F}^{(\vec{k}\,);\imath}(\vec{\epsilon}\,)
for
\imath=1,\dots,|\vec{k}\,|
which are independent of the mass parameter
m
. At this stage one should be able to exploit invariance under the
\mathbbm{Z}_{2}
-action
\epsilon_{3}\leftrightarrow-\epsilon_{123}
, together with the analytic behaviour of (
4.2
) in
\vec{\epsilon}\,
, to show that
\mathfrak{F}^{(\vec{k}\,);\imath}(\vec{\epsilon}\,)=\mathfrak{Z}_{[\mathbbm{C}^{2}/\mathbbm{Z}_{n}]\times\mathbbm{C}^{2}}^{\vec{k};\imath}(\vec{\epsilon}\,)
for all
\imath\in\{1,\dots,|\vec{k}\,|\}
.
